Picture : Charly TRIBALLEAU / AFP <p> UNITED NATIONS - The UN Security Council voted Friday in favour of a resolution backing Morocco 's autonomy plan for Western Sahara as the " most feasible " solution for the disputed territory , triggering celebrations in Rabat but angering Algeria . <p> Western Sahara is a vast mineral-rich former Spanish colony that is largely controlled by Morocco but has been claimed for decades by the pro-independence Polisario Front , which is supported by Algeria . <p> The Security Council had previously urged Morocco , the Polisario Front , Algeria and Mauritania to resume talks to reach a broad agreement . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Trump 's administration , the council shifted to support a plan , initially presented by Rabat in 2007 , in which Western Sahara would enjoy autonomy under Morocco 's sole sovereignty . <p> The resolution , adopted by an 11-0 vote with three abstentions and Algeria refusing to participate , said autonomy for Western Sahara under Moroccan sovereignty may be the basis for future negotiations to resolve the 50-year-old conflict . <p> " Genuine autonomy under Moroccan sovereignty could constitute a most feasible solution , " the UNSC resolution said . <p> Morocco 's King Mohammed VI lauded the vote as " historic , " saying " we are opening a new and victorious chapter in the process of enshrining the Moroccan character of the Sahara . " <p> It " fell short of the expectations and legitimate aspirations of the people of Western Sahara , represented by the Polisario Front ( who ) have been resisting for over 50 years to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ their own destiny , " he added . <p> The resolution adopted Friday calls on UN Secretary-General Antonio Guterres and de Mistura to conduct negotiations on the basis of the plan to reach a mutually acceptable agreement . <p> It also extends the UN peacekeeping mission in Western Sahara for another year .

@@130996456 <h> Israel : Five days in an apartheid state prison <p> On record : South African author Zukiswa Wanner was abducted by Israel from international waters with almost 500 others while attempting to break the over 18-year siege of Gaza . Photo : Supplied <p> In May 2023 , when I attended the Palestine Festival of Literature , I had a chance to observe Palestine and be a witness to the lives of Palestinians under apartheid and settler colonialism from the state of Israel . <p> This month , for five days , after being abducted by Israel from international waters with almost 500 others while attempting to break the over 18-year siege of Gaza to create a humanitarian corridor to allow foods , medication and baby formula in , I saw the face of the oppressor even more intimately than before . <p> From the moment of kidnapping , until we were freed on the border of Palestine and Jordan , I began in a way I could never have before , to understand South Africa 's apartheid history and perhaps , partial presence , in all its intimacy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Madikizela-Mandela 's 491 Days and Bongi Mkhabela 's Open Earth &amp; Black Roses . <p> After interception , being processed at Ashdod Port and refusing to be deported by Israel , my interrogator tells me I am being taken to prison . <p> My name is called and I am led out . A three-jailer party works in union with one cable-tying my hands in front , another blindfolding me and yet another cutting off the laces of my tekkies . Do they think I will commit suicide with my laces ? <p> I am walked to a bus and instructed to sit next to someone I ca n't see . I can hear voices and I want to see who is in there with me . My fear that I may get hit if I look to see who my neighbours are , is a little less than my curiosity so when I hear the door close , I pull down my blindfold and see my companions . I also realise I can remove my cable ties . <p> I am seated next to Yulia from Germany and they @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ I am scared that , in my T-shirt , I may die of hypothermia . The discomfort from the cold lingers as I type this . <p> After what seems to be hours later , one of the guards asks for four volunteers . I stand up as I am seated in front and Yulia follows . We have our blindfolds on so we do not know how many people have volunteered . <p> I have no idea what I am volunteering for . All I know is that I want to be away from this cold place . I recall thinking then , " I hope they are not taking us to somewhere that has opposite temperatures . " <p> Our blindfolds are removed and I find myself seated next to Iqbal from T ? rkiye , Yulia and a third person I think may have been Leila from the US . <p> I am tired . We all are . I wake up to a new day and the sun @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in the Naqab Desert . <p> We make no pretence of having our cable ties on and just hand them over to the new jailer who is cutting them from others . We are placed in a cage with a metal ceiling which looks like it can hold 50 comfortably but we are packed like sardines . <p> I am summoned from the cage by another guard with my folder . Another photo . I wish I had some lip gloss . I am taken to a metal container where I am instructed to remove all my clothes which will be numbered and " you will get your clothes when you leave " . I never get my clothes back . <p> The next visit is to the doctor and nurse whose idea of a health check is perfunctory . Height , weight , blood pressure . I ca n't believe American taxpayers pay for this level of basics . <p> Then we are taken to the cells . <p> The cells overlook a 400 square metre cemented field with lines , I will hear two days later , when @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are not supposed to walk out of . @@130996879 <h> Silence is complicity <h> It has been a traumatic and deadly decade for journalists , and the International Day to End Impunity for Crimes Against Journalists on 2 November is an opportunity to raise global awareness on justice , safety and freedom for our colleagues . <p> It is the largest massacre of journalists in human history . In Gaza , 253 Palestinian journalists and media workers have been killed since October 2023 -- more than those killed in the wars of Afghanistan , Vietnam , the Balkans , World War I , and World War II combined . Yet these killings remain mostly uninvestigated , unpunished , and unacknowledged by those responsible -- a stark example of the impunity that allows attacks on journalists to continue . <p> Ten of those 253 were my colleagues at Al Jazeera Media Network . I still miss the voice of my colleague , Anas Al-Sharif , every morning from Gaza City -- reporting on the humanitarian situation , talking to people every day . <p> He was killed in a targeted attack on journalists near Al Shifa Hospital on 10 August @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ be without a father , and a farewell letter that went viral -- a heartbreaking tradition now common among Gaza 's journalists , who leave their last words in hospitals to be delivered to loved ones after their death . <h> Not accidents of war <p> No other media organisation has ever lost so many journalists in such a short time , in any single conflict . These are reporters and camerapersons who have been working under inhuman conditions for a prolonged period : bombed , starved , displaced , hunted and threatened . <p> Their killings are not accidents of war but deliberate state policy -- and the whole world is watching it live on their phones . Their work should be celebrated as heroic . Were it not for them , the world might never have known what has been happening in Gaza , since Israel allows no foreign journalists to enter unless embedded with the Israeli army and reporting under its scrutiny . It is still the case today , after the ceasefire agreement has entered in place . <p> Journalists have been inheriting gear from each @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ their vest and resumes their work -- sometimes on the same day , reporting on the killing of their colleague while wearing that very vest . <h> Driven by duty to tell their stories <p> One particular vest is now being worn by a third journalist , after two were consecutively killed wearing it . They know the risk they face , yet they continue -- driven by duty to tell the world their people 's stories , and by the conviction that their lives as Palestinians are already under threat , so they might as well use the time left to make a difference . <p> I remember our first loss in Gaza in December 2023 : Samer Abu Daqqa . He was killed while documenting the aftermath of an airstrike . Wounded and trapped , he bled for hours as medics were blocked from reaching him . We kept pleading with the world on all platforms to save him . <p> We knew he was still alive . When colleagues later recovered his camera , it was still recording -- smoke and silence filling the final frame . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Al Jazeera 's newsroom into a mourning hall and sent a clear message : journalists in Gaza were now deliberate targets , exactly like the 8 April 2003 when our newsroom turned into a mourning hall as we lost our colleague Tarek Ayyoub , killed in a US airstrike on Baghdad , Iraq . The aim is always the same : to silence the voice of the truth . <p> In Palestine this impunity had begun way before the genocidal war on Gaza -- when my colleague and friend Shireen Abu Akleh was killed by a sniper in May 2022 while reporting from the Jenin refugee camp in the occupied West Bank . <p> She was a dual Palestinian-American citizen . Dozens of investigations proved , time and again , that she was targeted -- yet there were no consequences . That lack of accountability allowed Israel to continue and intensify its killing of journalists without fear of repercussions . <p> It has even gone further , labelling journalists as " terrorists " to justify their murder . <h> Betraying their profession <p> Sadly , some media organisations echoed this @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Israel 's claims . The dehumanisation of Palestinians by the media extended to Palestinian journalists , who were not considered " white " enough to be defended in the name of " universal " human rights . <p> If the community of journalists does not defend its own -- regardless of colour , race , nationality , ethnicity or religion -- then Israel 's war on journalists will inevitably become a war on journalism itself . If this massacre is allowed to continue in Gaza today , unpunished and unaccounted for , it can happen anywhere , anytime , again . <p> Silence is complicity . <p> Silence is complicity . <p> Dima Khatib is the managing director of the award-winning AJ+ channels in four languages : English , Arabic , Spanish and French . She joined the Network in 1997 as a junior broadcast journalist in Qatar and went on to become the first female executive within Al Jazeera . <p> Dima Khatib is the Managing Director of the award-winning AJ+ channels in four languages : English , Arabic , Spanish and French . She joined the Network in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on to become the first female executive within Al Jazeera . Later , Dima set up bureaus in China and Venezuela where she was Al Jazeera 's bureau chief for almost a decade . Dima has reported from over 30 countries in 5 continents , interviewing presidents and people from all walks of life . Dima is a polyglot who has published the book , ' Love Refugee ' . She has been classified as one of the most influential Arabs online since the early days of social media . <h> ABOUT US <p> The Media Online is the definitive online point of reference for South Africa 's media industry offering relevant , focused and topical news on the media sector . @@131012028 <p> We are not refugees . We reject the narrative that portrays Afrikaners as victims of racial persecution in post-apartheid South Africa . The Trump administration 's plan to prioritize white South Africans for refugee status , while drastically cutting overall refugee admissions , have brought our identity into the spotlight in ways that are deeply troubling . <p> We reject the narrative that casts Afrikaners as victims of racial persecution in post-apartheid South Africa . This framing , now being used to support the far-right " Great Replacement " theory in the United States , is not only misleading , but also dangerous @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ our history , and reduces a complex social context and necessary levelling of playing fields into a simplistic symbol of white decline . <p> --&gt; <p> Let us be clear : South Africa faces serious challenges -- crime , inequality , and the enduring legacy of apartheid . But these issues affect South Africans of all races . To cherry-pick white suffering and elevate it above others is dishonest and harmful . It feeds extremist ideologies that perpetuate division and have inspired real-world violence , including mass shootings . <p> We are not pawns in America 's culture wars . We are South Africans , part of a diverse , evolving nation still grappling with its past and striving toward a more just future . Our identity is not monolithic , nor is it defined by animosity between historical groupings . Afrikaners have done harm in the past , and we acknowledge that . As citizens of post-apartheid South Africa , we have dedicated ourselves to building our country . Singling us out as victims of multiracialism alienates us from our fellow-South Africans and harms relationships that have been fostered @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ most is the hijacking of our ethnic identity by fringe groups - both in South Africa and abroad . Projects like " Make Afrikaners Great Again " do not represent us . They misrepresent our history and misappropriate our culture to promote exclusionary and racialized ideological agendas . We refuse to let organisations such as Afriforum and the Solidarity Movement speak for us -- no single organisation has a clear or legitimate mandate to speak on behalf of all Afrikaners . <p> Afrikaners like us , who trace our South African roots to the 17th and 18th centuries choose to call ourselves members of an inclusive and diverse Afrikaans-speaking community . We share a language , and our cultural traditions and religious roots are the same , but our community holds a diversity of views . This diversity is entrenched in our folklore and it is our strength , not our weakness . South Africa 's diversity enriches us . <p> --&gt; <p> The idea that white South Africans deserve special asylum status because of their race undermines the very principles of refugee protection . Vulnerability - not race -should @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ others is to reinforce a racialized worldview that elevates whiteness above others and sees white identity as under existential threat . This is not a reflection of our values or our lived experience . Calling crime in South Africa ' white genocide ' at a moment when Gaza and Sudan are showing in real time what that actually means , is crass and narcissistic . <p> This response is signed by Afrikaners who believe in truth , justice , and the importance of resisting the misuse of history . @@131131220 <h> OPINION <h> The SA-US Relationship : A response to Mills and Hartley ( I ) <p> James Hamill <p> 06 November 2025 <p> James Hamill takes issue with the idea that all responsibility for current discord rests with Pretoria <p> Greg Mills and Ray Hartley have made interesting and welcome contributions to the debates on South African politics over a number of years , but their articles on South Africa 's foreign policy for Politicsweb over the course of 2025 have become regrettably one-dimensional . They have also drifted into an inconsistency which , at times , has spilled over into outright hypocrisy . <p> The central theme of their articles on the South Africa-United States relationship is that South Africa is threatening its own interests with behaviour that has needlessly antagonised the US . This has now triggered a predictable backlash from President Donald Trump 's administration in the form of steep 30% tariffs ( the highest in sub-Saharan Africa ) , a suspension of US aid , and a series of diplomatic snubs designed to express its disapproval of Ramaphosa 's government and to undermine South Africa @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ message from these articles is clear enough : South Africa has foolishly prioritised an ideologically driven , anti-Western , agenda through its hostility to Israel and its relationship with Iran , whereas only a strong focus on economic self-interest should be driving policy . If South African policies are antagonising the Trump administration and , by extension , are endangering US trade , investment and aid then they should be jettisoned forthwith . <p> Thus their advice to the government that it should withdraw from the case it has brought against Israel at the International Court of Justice ( ICJ ) under the 1948 Genocide Convention , reduce its links with Iran and generally fall into line with US foreign policy requirements. 1 Realpolitik should take precedence over ideology or morally driven concerns , which is certainly how the Ramaphosa government views its ICJ case . <p> These are considered to be luxuries which a small to middling state in the global economy like South Africa can ill afford , particularly given its sluggish rate of growth and unacceptably high level of unemployment . There is certainly a good argument @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ where the closeness of that relationship makes no sense from either a realpolitik or moral perspective given Western hostility to Iran , South Africa 's relatively modest economic relationship with Tehran , and the fact that the Iranian regime 's philosophy and actions are the antithesis of the values underpinning the South African constitution . <p> It is particularly lamentable to find the African National Congress ( ANC ) and South African Communist Party cheerleading for Iran while blithely ignoring the regime 's physical liquidation of the organised left in that country , its appalling record on trade union freedoms and women 's rights , added to its persecution of homosexuals and the people of the Baha'i faith . <p> --&gt; <p> Even if a legitimate argument can be made that America 's enemies are not automatically those of South Africa , enlightened self-interest should be driving South Africa towards a more considered and pragmatic position in which it places greater distance between itself and Iran . <p> South Africa : One US target among many <p> That said , what is deeply problematic about the Mills and Hartley approach @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ current discord with Washington is entirely the responsibility of South Africa with an exasperated , but essentially blameless , US merely responding to Pretoria 's supposedly delinquent behaviour . A virtually identical argument has been made by William Saunderson-Meyer in his articles for Politicsweb on US-South Africa relations throughout 2025 . <p> All responsibility for the current crisis in the relationship rests with South Africa . 2 <p> --&gt; <p> This argument would carry much greater weight if South Africa was some kind of outlier or rogue state in global politics whose behaviour is generally agreed to be uniquely awful . In that case , Washington 's attack on it might be presented as a justified , if isolated , episode in a generally stable and constructive US approach to international relations . <p> It is actually nothing of the sort , however . For example , South Africa has not found itself out on a limb due to its position on Gaza and it is arrant nonsense to suggest otherwise . It is , in fact , positioned firmly within the global consensus on that issue , unlike the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is facing a deepening isolation on account of its behaviour in Gaza. 3 <p> What has also become evident over the last year is that South Africa is just one US target among many . The assault on it fits a now well-established pattern of US coercion and intimidation of states deemed to be acting contrary to its interests , who do not view global politics through precisely the same lens as Washington , or whose very statehood and sovereignty offends Trump . <p> Unsurprisingly , that aggressive approach is now alienating many states well beyond South Africa , particularly Brazil and India - though less so Russia , a subject worthy of an extended discussion in its own right - as Trump uses tariffs as an economic and political battering ram against any states he considers obstructive . Brazil , for example , denounced Trump 's ' tariff blackmail ' when he imposed punitive 50% tariffs on the country despite the fact the US runs a large trade surplus with that country. 4 <p> --&gt; <p> There is no serious economic rationale for any of this ; instead , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ character . The real targets here are Brazil 's support for South Africa 's ICJ case ( which it joined in July 2025 ) and Brazilian democracy per se . The Brazilian electorate is being punished for choosing to elect a government ( led by Lula da Silva ) of which Trump disapproves instead of re-electing his ally , Jair Bolsonaro , who , like Trump , is a populist demagogue with deeply authoritarian instincts . Bolsonaro refused to accept his defeat at the ballot box in 2022 , was subsequently charged with seeking to foment a coup d'etat and was found guilty in September 2025 and sentenced to 27 years imprisonment. 5 <p> His post-election behaviour was drawn directly from Trump 's own playbook when , between November 2020 and January 2021 , Trump claimed , without a shred of supporting evidence , that the US presidential election had been ' stolen . ' He then attempted to subvert the will of the electorate and American democracy itself , even to the point of encouraging insurrectionist mob violence against the Capitol itself on 6 January 2021 in an attempt @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ As the political scientists Filipe Campante and Steven Levitsky stated , ' the Brazilian Supreme Court did what the US Senate and federal courts tragically failed to do : bring a former president who assaulted democracy to justice . ' 6 <p> That is why we are witnessing the current US attack on Brazil 's independent democratic institutions , most notably the sanctions he has imposed upon its senior judiciary but it is to Brazil 's great credit that it is robustly defending its democracy and its sovereignty and is not buckling to Trump 's aggressive interference . That Trump was threatening the Argentine electorate with economic retribution should it too decide it had had enough of his ally , President Javier Milei , ahead of mid-term elections there , was as predictable as it was malevolent. 7 <p> --&gt; <p> Equally , India is being punished -- also with 50% tariffs - for its failure to automatically align with US foreign policy positions and for committing the , apparently heinous , offence of not massaging Trump 's huge ego by agreeing that he was indispensable to the cessation of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ threatened with unilateral and illegal US military action on its sovereign territory . <p> Trump 's ignorance of Africa is legendary as is his addiction to crude and simplistic analyses of - and solutions for - complex problems . So it was no surprise to hear him state in November 2025 that the US ' may very well go into that now disgraced country , guns-a-blazing to completely wipe out Islamic terrorists who are committing these horrible atrocities ' thereby preventing a ' mass slaughter ' of Christians in the country. 8 <p> One can only marvel at the mindset that considers this to be a good idea . <p> Nor is US hostility confined to adversaries or formally non-aligned states . Long-standing American allies , such as the member states of the European Union , have also been subjected to Trump 's bullying and erratic behaviour as he sets about dismantling a post-war order previously anchored by the US . Denmark has objected to Trump 's predatory designs on the territory of Greenland9 and Panama has been threatened with illegal US action to reestablish its control of the Panama @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Trump told his audience bluntly , ' we 're taking it back . ' 11 Colombia , the US 's principal ally for the last three decades in the fight against narcotics in Latin America , is now being threatened with the use of illegal military force if it does not fully stamp out drug production in the country , an unachievable objective . <p> Trump has already authorised a number of military strikes against vessels in international waters in the Caribbean and Pacific where he asserts a US right to be judge , prosecutor , jury and executioner. 12 Meanwhile , Canada , one of Washington 's oldest allies , has had its very existence as a sovereign state questioned in a series of outrageous Trump outbursts in which he has called for it to become the 51st American state . This has been followed by a US economic squeeze on the country. 13 <p> Considering the office he holds , it is deeply disturbing that Trump appears to have no understanding that , under international law , borders may only be changed peacefully and by the mutual @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ warfare , threats of annexation or actual military aggression . This perhaps explains why he has been so willing to see large chunks of Ukraine 's sovereign territory ceded to the Putin regime . <p> Unless Mills , Hartley and Saunderson-Meyer are inviting us to believe that Brazil , Canada , Colombia , Denmark , India , Nigeria , Panama and South Africa , to name just some of the states who have found themselves in Trump 's crosshairs , are all delinquents and have all invited US punishment -- something of a stretch -- then perhaps the time has arrived to recognise the common denominator in these ongoing disputes , namely , Donald Trump and his extremist , threatening and inflammatory behaviour . <p> Unfortunately , however , a serious critique of US foreign policy under Trump is entirely missing from their articles . Consequently , it is only ever Pretoria which has a ' partisan agenda ' and which is to be considered ' obdurate ' , ' radical ' or ' provocative ' and therefore it is only Pretoria which is required to ' soften its approach @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ example of this came with the controversy around South Africa 's appointment of Ebrahim Rasool as its ambassador to Washington who was then declared persona non grata by the Trump administration in March 2025 . Rasool 's appointment was considered an antagonistic move as he was a critic of Trump with Mills and Hartley claiming he was ' linked to radicalism . ' xiv <p> Contrast this with their response to Trump 's nomination of Brent Bozell as the US ambassador to South Africa ( also in March 2025 ) which has been greeted with a monastic silence on their part . Bozell has no diplomatic experience but is a far right ideologue , a critic of Nelson Mandela and of the ANC both during the apartheid era and since . He also publicly endorsed Trump 's Goebbels grade big lie about a ' stolen election ' in 2020 . <p> Yet this nomination failed to generate any critical comment from Mills and Hartley ; there was no suggestion that Bozell was ' linked to radicalism ' or that his nomination was in any way provocative . Nor have we @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ or to change its tone . Unfortunately , this hypocrisy is now standard practice with even the mildest criticism of Trump 's behaviour considered to be off-limits . US policy demands , however belligerent and unreasonable , are to be treated with a fawning deference as though handed down on tablets of stone , immovable and unchallengeable . <p> Thus , for Mills and Hartley , ' it does n't really matter if what Trump has been briefed on is 100% accurate . What matters is he believes what he believes and is taking action . ' xv One can only imagine the withering scorn such an astonishing sentence would have drawn from them if the name Trump was replaced by Ramaphosa , Lula , Putin , Xi or Modi . Trump has always churned out lies on an industrial scale -- it is his defining characteristic - and exposing and challenging them matters hugely , or at least it should to commentators in a democratic society . <p> Yet Mills and Hartley help normalise this behaviour by treating those lies as if they were trifling , minor details with @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ promptly with demands based on those very same falsehoods . This provides another sobering reminder of the relative ease with which some mainstream conservatives swiftly adapt to , and make their accommodations and shabby compromises with , the dogmatic , authoritarian populism of the far right , rather than choosing to confront it . <p> Realpolitik : now you see it , now you do n't <p> Revealingly , in August 2025 in Politicsweb , Mills and Hartley condemned what they called South Africa 's ' kow-towing ' to China on the issue of Taiwan through its proposed relocation of Taiwan 's liaison office from Pretoria to Johannesburg . They then urged South Africa to stand up to China with the Czech Republic 's robust approach apparently serving as a model. xvi <p> Yet , simultaneously , they were advocates of a quite obsequious posture toward the US and the abandonment of domestic and foreign policies at Washington 's behest - or ' kow-towing ' , to use their own term . For Mills and Hartley , when the US demands South Africa jump , the only permissible response from @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> This has even extended to them welcoming Trump 's economic pressure as a lever to help reverse South African domestic and foreign policies to which they take exception - or a ' legislative spring clean ' , as they euphemistically labelled it. xvii One might have fondly imagined that in a sovereign , democratic state , policy changes were an internal matter and the prerogative of South African voters via the ballot box . <p> Following elections , it should then fall to parliament and the executive to implement the electorate 's will . However , democracy always carries the risk that the voters , parliament or the executive will not automatically comply with their wishes and so , for Mills and Hartley , external US pressure is viewed as the means of delivering change . The double standards here are glaring . <p> Moral positions ( on Gaza ) must be subordinated to arguments rooted in realpolitik , the national interest and economic wellbeing in the case of the US ( South Africa 's second largest trade partner ) but must then be prioritised over economic wellbeing , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ( despite China being , by some distance , South Africa 's largest and most important trading partner ) . <p> The ' surrender of South Africa 's sovereign interests and responsibilities to Beijing ' xviii is to be deplored while the same writers actively canvass for the ' surrender of South Africa 's sovereign interests and responsibilities ' to the US . This is all a very long way from the ' active non-alignment ' they profess to endorse , as well as being incoherent and contradictory. xix It also confirms that hypocrisy and selective moralising on international relations and foreign policy is far from being confined to Ramaphosa 's ANC . 
@@131156980 <h> Israel : Five Days in an Apartheid State Prison <p> Zukiswa Wanner turned the table on her Israeii captors and made life hell for them . <p> Consulate officials often come and visit their citizens . Our hearts are with our American and German comrades because we know they will not find sympathetic officials . <p> Whenever any of us are called to see our consular officials , we tell them to check the time . Meals and the consulate visits are our only way of knowing the time . <p> As can be imagined , we have a lot of free time so in addition to the singing and being general nuisances , we sleep a lot . <p> Being in Cell 4 and the whole block in general is a lot like a retreat for delinquent teenage girls . Mikako from Japan , at 63 and oldest after Josefina has left , leads us in stretches . <p> I prompt a story that everyone must continue . <p> My beginning , " They came for Josefina at 2 o'clock in the morning . They left me there @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ... " I wish I could tell you we created a story worthy of a Booker or adaptation to Netflix . Alas , no . But we have lots of laughs with each addition to the story as it degenerates . <p> We are also , in turns , taken to see ' the judge ' who asks again whether we want to leave or stay . <p> We soon realise that it makes little difference because people with health problems who signed to leave after 72 hours detention are still with those of us who did not sign . <p> They call me on the second day to see the judge . As we wait to be taken to the holding cells/cages before seeing the judge without a lawyer , Yasmin , who is part of the steering committee for the Global Sumud Flotilla , starts questioning one of the guards standing with us why they kill babies and starve people . He smiles indulgently , but then I say something which gets him really riled up . <p> I say ever so politely , " Sir . Do @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ kill and maim and imprison people , but you ca n't make anyone love you . We all hate you . The whole world hates you . " Somehow this seems to get to him because he looks at me furiously and shouts , " Shut up . Do n't push it . " I shut up , but the point has been made . <p> We go and see the judge without a lawyer . Four of us at the same time , the Brazilian will see a different judge . <p> " Would you like us to send you back home to your country ? " Our answer is the same . <p> We did not come here voluntarily . We were abducted from international waters and we want to complete our mission . I use that time to drink water as I have little trust in their tap water . <p> We go back to our cells and the counting , and the songs of freedom continue . <p> On Sunday , Cell 4 is finally let out to take a shower and have some exercise . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ sink but rather looking forward to the shower . I give myself five minutes walking in the open space and this is when I learn I am not supposed to walk outside the line when the guard above me yells and points his gun at me . <p> I am convinced now , more than ever , that all functionaries of Apartheid Israel are incapable of speaking in a regular voice . It ca n't be polite to talk that loudly on stolen land , surely ? <p> During the night , someone in Cell 5 with heart problems needed a doctor . The agitators of Cell 4 spent much of last night asking for a doctor for Cell 5 . No doctor came . <p> Now , outside , when our 20 minutes are up , we sit down between Cell 4 and Cell 5 , refuse to go back in our cell until a doctor comes for Cell 5 . If only one of us had done this , we would probably have been beaten and thrown into solitary confinement or something . <p> But because we have @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ we only do so and get back in our cell when a medic comes . Back in the cell , we soon start calling out the guards again . <p> We read that prisoners are supposed to have an hour outside , and the smokers are supposed to get cigarettes yet the smokers have not been given cigarettes , and we only got 20 minutes outside . Let us out . <p> The guards can not believe our chutzpah . One of them answers , " You are not prisoners . You are activists . " <p> Yasumara Mikako <p> Oh ? <p> Later that afternoon , Carrie , Zaheera , Fatima and I are called . Four days after interception , and three days since arriving at K'tziot Prison , we are finally going to see our consulate officials . <p> When we get to the cage where we are meeting them , they introduce themselves and suggest that we wait for Mandla and Reaaz . As we wait , the consul officials inform us that they left Jerusalem at 8 in the morning , have been at Ktzi'ot @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> When the guys finally join us , it 's like a family reunion . Just as we are presenting our case to the officials , one of the Israelis tells us its time up . Two minutes is what we have had . It is bizarre . <p> The officials seem ready to acquiesce but we collectively make noise and ask for 10 minutes . The Israelis finally agree to five minutes extra . We leave disappointed with the meeting because the officials sounded as though they were leaving it all in the hands of Israelis in spite of Department of International Relations and Cooperation knowing that we would be undertaking this mission more than a month before and an interception was a possibility if we did not make it to Gaza . <p> " We have a WhatsApp group with the Department , the Presidency , your families and SA BDS Coalition . " As Nigerian mothers would say , " clap for yourself . " <p> In the early evening with more people having been taken out , Cell 4 is broken up . Mikako and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ We go to Cell 13 where we are now with some South Africans and Mexicans and a Swede , Greta Thunberg . <p> Of all the women arrested , she has been at the receiving end of the most violence from the Israelis . And yet she is strangely calm and polite . I was never this calm at 22 . <p> The Mexicans get a call through a sympathetic guard from their ambassador to check on how they are doing . There and then , the South Africans decide that the Mexican Embassy represents us . <p> We are moved to Cell 10 on Monday . There are seven of us in the cell . Four South Africans and three Mexicans . The Mexicans are called to meet their consul officials . <p> When they return , they inform us that their officials told them that they will be dropped at King Hussein Bridge by Israeli officials the next day . The South Africans sigh longingly . To be as informed as the Mexicans . Not knowing can lead to not worrying but sometimes too , it can lead @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ embassy is in Ramallah , considered Palestinian territory and South Africa , given the ICJ and ICC case , is considered hostile by the apartheid state but surely a message could have been passed through a friendly embassy ? <p> Mexico , maybe ? But we were unnecessarily worried . In the late afternoon , we are called as a cell for the doctor 's visit . We now know that we shall be leaving together with the Mexicans . <p> At four in the morning , the security guards wake us up and allow us to shower . We leave our cell 30 minutes later and are on the buses . <p> And as the buses leave with their high windows where we can not be seen , we are able to stand and see Gaza in the horizon . So near yet so far . We know that , as we see it , last night , maybe even now , more Palestinians are mourning their families murdered by a bloodthirsty apartheid regime . <p> On this bus with these women from different countries united in love for @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Palestinian , and our collective freedom . <p> In this -- the second and last instalment of South African author Zukiswa Wanner 's account of their abduction by Israeli authorities , she tells the story of how she and other members of the Flotilla to Gaza cheekily cocked a snook at their captors , instead of suffering peacefully , as was expected of them by Netanyahu 's forces . 
@@131169798 <h> OPINION <h> The Kumbaya coalition <p> William Saunderson-Meyer says a GNU that allows the ANC 's failed policies to prevail is not a coalition but a co-option <p> A FAMOUS GROUSE <p> One has to hand it to Cyril . He conducts , like a maestro , the impromptu and motley 10-part ensemble that stands between him and political oblivion , and between South Africa and economic disaster . <p> Last weekend , without warning them of the agenda , President Ramaphosa mysteriously summoned the leaders of the nine political parties that , along with the ANC , comprise the Government of National Unity ( GNU ) to a secret two-day meeting . It was the first meeting between the GNU leaders in 177 days , according to Rapport . He called it a ' strategic retreat ' , his new label for one of those touchy-feely talk-shops that his presidential predecessors called a ' bosberaad ' or a ' lekgotla ' , from Afrikaans and Zulu , respectively . <p> The name might have changed , but the Kumbaya spirit of ' rainbow nation ' days is undimmed @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ' there were ' moments of quiet and nice joy ' , while RISE Mzansi leader Songezo Zibi called it a time of ' collaboration and reflection ' . The PA 's Gayton McKenzie clearly found it a transcendental experience , saying the spirit of unity in the GNU is now of ' holy ' proportions . <p> An unidentified party leader quoted by News24 , waxed Biblical , too . It had been ' an opportunity to break bread and share wine together ' . There were ' no sticking points ' ; they ' had a lovely dinner together ' , watched cricket on the big screen and , as he put it , the ' wine was flowing ' . <p> In all the rapturous news reports on this love-fest at the Cradle of Humankind -- the world 's largest concentration of human ancestral remains and ' a place of learning , reflection , and inspiration ' according to its advertising -- he then strikes the only sour note . The reason for it being constructive was because everyone put South Africa 's interest first , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ' . John Steenhuisen had ' refrained ' from raising the DA 's ' contentious positions ' on matters such as the Basic Education Laws Amendment ( BELA ) Act , the National Health Insurance ( NHI ) Act , the Broad-Based Black Economic Empowerment ( B-BBEE ) Act , and other court challenges . <p> --&gt; <p> ' What is problematic of the DA , ' he continued , ' is that they like to raise these issues outside of the GNU . They raise them in billboards in Johannesburg , media and press statements , but within the GNU , they do n't raise them . This is why I said there were no tensions in the room , because John did n't bring any into the room . He was collaborative , speaking about the diversification of trade and opportunities that he 's now finding in China , and he never thought that he would fall in love with China . He was a very different party leader . He did n't raise any of the issues the DA has around economic policy . ' <p> This @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ inadvertently exposes the GNU 's greatest flaw : a dearth of robustly contesting ideas . South Africa is ill served by a GNU in which the opposition voices are so chuffed simply to be invited to be at the table that they bite their tongues instead of challenging their ANC host . A GNU that allows the ANC 's failed policies to prevail is not a coalition but a co-option . <p> Operational improvements are n't enough . The DA -- the GNU 's second-largest party -- boasts about quicker visas at Home Affairs and tidier Public Works leases , but unless opposition members of the GNU force substantial policy shifts soon -- not after years of legal trench warfare -- on expropriation without compensation , B-BBEE hurdles for foreign investors and race quotas for local employers , cadre deployment , and the government 's anti-Western tilt in foreign and defence policy , South Africa is as surely ' doomed ' as it would be under a widely feared ANC ' Doomsday coalition ' with the EFF . <p> So , the question is whether Ramaphosa 's strategic retreat has @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , rather than the less controversial operational matters ? The short answer is maybe , but not much . <p> --&gt; <p> Much of the post-meeting communiqu ? was devoted to the GNU 's adoption of the Medium-Term Development Plan ( MTDP ) as its ' transformative vision ' through to 2029 . There 's little new here . The MTDP largely reheats the National Development Plan 's goals of 2012 , served up at regular intervals since , most recently in the ANC 's 10-point economic plan unveiled in October . The one modest advance is a delivery schedule -- Operation Vulindlela Phase II , working with the private sector -- plus an MTDP results framework and a set of intervention indicators to track drift or progress . <p> The communiqu ? references the long-promised GNU ' clearing-house ' -- a policy dispute-resolution panel to mediate and , where needed , decide between GNU partners . It was promised a year ago and is only now taking shape . Even in this Kumbaya coalition , it wo n't lack for work : cadre deployment and foreign policy alone will @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for ' greater urgency and action ' on the ' further professionalisation and depoliticisation of the public service ' and minimum standards for board appointments to state-owned entities . For the DA , which is litigating the issue , that should mean the end of ANC cadre deployment . For the ANC , which would be destroyed by losing this lever of patronage and control , it plainly does not . Ramaphosa , as ever , wants both : he dreams of an efficient civil service while passionately defending cadre deployment . It 's a contradiction tailor-made for the clearing-house . <p> Foreign affairs will be the next flashpoint . As Steenhuisen told Daily Maverick , the DA wants a genuinely non-aligned , non-ideological course . That goes to the ANC 's ideological DNA and , it seems , that of some opposition leaders . As that earlier cited anonymous leader told News24 : ' We know that some parties have different views around South Africa 's role in several conflicts . <p> --&gt; <p> We certainly agreed that South Africa has a role to play in driving a human @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in ANC parlance , a ' human rights approach ' means denouncing Israel in Gaza while tiptoeing around Islamist slaughter in Sudan , and habitually shielding Iran , Russia and China in multilateral forums . In the meanwhile , the leaders have agreed at the weekend to meet again for ' further engagement on SA 's international relations to develop a common understanding of the national interest ' . <p> Beyond the MTDP , the communiqu ? rolled out the usual promises -- a food-security plan , youth skills and work experience , tougher action on organised crime , and stronger interventions in failing municipalities -- the boilerplate stuff that you 'll find in every party 's manifesto . It 's not the nitty-gritty stuff that needs to be tackled . <p> Whatever the public communications ' emphasis on the unity between the leaders , there obviously remain many lurking obstacles . @@131228469 <h> OPINION <h> Francesca Albanese : A reply to the SAJBD <p> Mervyn Bennun <p> 09 November 2025 <p> Mervyn Bennun says that being Jewish means standing with the victims of Zionist violence and with Palestinians <p> I should like to respond to the statement by Wendy Kahn , the National Director writing on behalf of the South African Jewish Board of Deputies ( SAJBD ) . The statement is on the occasion of the visit to South Africa by Francesca Albanese , the United Nations Special Rapporteur on the situation of human rights in the Palestinian territory occupied since 1967 . <p> I am Jewish and a member of the South African Jews for a Free Palestine and the Palestine Solidarity Campaign , but I am not their spokesperson . I support them because they avoid violence in their antizionist campaigning in South Africa . <p> Francesca Albanese had been invited to deliver the annual Nelson Mandela Memorial Lecture at the invitation of the Nelson Mandela Foundation . The reaction of the SAJBD as a Zionist body is worth noting because Zionism needs violence . Zionism will even do @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> ' Even after Hamas 's October 7 , 2023 massacre , DIRCO released statements that blamed Israel for the attack and ignored the Jewish civilians who were murdered , raped or abducted that day , failing to condemn the act of terrorism ' . <p> In fact , the South African Department of International Relations and Cooperation ( DIRCO ) issued a statement that very day -- on 7 October 2023 : <p> --&gt; <p> ' South Africa calls for the immediate cessation of violence , restraint , and peace between Israel and Palestine . <p> ' South Africa expresses its grave concern over the recent devastating escalation in the Israeli-Palestinian conflict . The new conflagration has arisen from the continued illegal occupation of Palestine land , continued settlement expansion , desecration of the Al Aqsa mosque and Christian holy sites , and ongoing oppression of the Palestinian people . The region is in desperate need of a credible peace process that delivers on the calls of a plethora of previous UN resolutions for a two-state solution and a just and comprehensive peace between Israel and Palestine ' . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on behalf of the SAJBD , has fairly reflected the DIRCO statement . It conveniently introduces , however , a closer examination of why the SAJBD describes Francesca Albanese as ' antisemitic ' . <p> She is the United Nations Special Rapporteur on the Occupied Palestinian Territories . In this capacity , she reports her findings to the Secretary-General of the United Nations who transmits her Reports to the UN General Assembly . It is in this context that Wendy Kahn and Zionists generally conclude that she is antisemitic . <p> --&gt; <p> In her first Report , given in September 2022 on the ' Situation of human rights in the Palestinian Territories occupied since 1967 ' , she wrote that she ' addresses a number of concerns pertaining to the situation of human rights in the West Bank , including East Jerusalem , and Gaza and presents an in-depth analysis of the right of the Palestinian people to self-determination ' . She commences by reporting that the Permanent Representative of Israel declined to meet her and that she was denied access to the occupied Palestinian territory including East Jerusalem @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ research and analysis , and enhanced by consultations and submissions ' . <p> What follows is a highly-detailed study of the laws and the circumstances of the indigenous inhabitants of the Occupied Palestinian Territories . Israel maintains that it complies with international law , and indeed it has signed the Charter of the United Nations of which it is a member . Article 1(2) of the Charter states that the purpose of the United Nations is : <p> ' To develop friendly relations among nations based on respect for the principle of equal rights and self-determination of peoples , and to take other appropriate measures to strengthen universal peace ; ' <p> ..... <p> --&gt; <p> Francesca Albanese 's study is therefore based on this premise . What follows is a close study of the subject , citing the authorities relied on . @@131264764 <h> OPINION <h> The SA-US relationship : A response to Mills &amp; Hartley ( II ) <p> James Hamill <p> 10 November 2025 <p> James Hamill says rather than focusing on SA , the authors should turn their fire on the Trump administration and its abuses <p> The previous article challenged the assumptions on which Greg Mills and Ray Hartley based their analysis of the South Africa-US relationship . The Integrated Food Security Phase Classification ( IPC ) has announced that famine has been detected in the regions of El-Fasher and Kadugli . The UN-backed global hunger monitor has also warned of a rising famine in Darfur and other regions . <p> The paramilitary Rapid Support Forces ( RSF ) seized the city of El-Fasher , killing over 450 people in a hospital and carrying out ethnically targeted killings and sexual assaults . More than 14 million people have been displaced , and two regions are experiencing famine , as reported by Al Jazeera and UN agencies . According to the UN , more than 150,000 people have been killed so far . Given these observations , it 's paramount for South Africa , as the G20 President , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the Sudanese leadership accountable , whether civilian , military , or otherwise . <p> In the past , the AU has played a vital role in conflict resolution across the continent , deploying peacekeeping missions to stabilize conflict zones , such as AMISOM in Somalia and UNAMID in Darfur . The AU has also facilitated negotiations between warring parties , promoting dialogue and reconciliation . Additionally , the AU prevents conflicts through early warning systems , diplomatic efforts , and addressing root causes . The AU Peace and Security Council ( PSC ) oversees conflict resolution efforts , deploying missions and imposing sanctions when necessary . <p> The question remains : why is the AU not taking a similar stance in the ongoing conflict in Sudan ? <p> The United Nations Secretary-General , Ant ? nio Guterres , has warned of a humanitarian catastrophe . @@131304908 <p> The SACP has criticised the government 's proposed new economic framework , labelling it the " Same aGAIN " strategy . / Gallo Images <p> The South African Communist Party ( SACP ) has launched a searing critique of the government 's proposed new economic framework , labelling it the " Same aGAIN " strategy and warning it will perpetuate the unemployment , poverty , and inequality that have become hallmarks of the post-apartheid economy . <p> In a statement issued after a recent Politburo meeting , the SACP , a nominal member of the ruling governing alliance , argued that the leaked Growth and Inclusion ( GAIN ) strategy represents a continuation of the neoliberal policies adopted since 1996 . The party claims these policies have primarily served the interests of international finance capital and a domestic capitalist elite at the expense of the working-class majority . <h> Mounting discontent <p> The intervention highlights the profound ideological fractures within South Africa 's government and underscores the mounting discontent with economic policies that have failed to dismantle one of the world 's most starkly unequal societies . With an @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , the SACP argues that tinkering within the same economic paradigm is a recipe for continued social crisis . <p> " Far from being transformative , the GAIN strategy reproduces the same old economic framework , " the Politburo stated . " It is the same as before , merely repackaged and rebranded . Hence the SACP 's description of the GAIN strategy as the ' Same aGAIN ' . " <p> The party 's critique focuses on what it sees as the strategy 's core failings . These are a commitment to fiscal consolidation , which it equates with austerity . An accelerated push for privatisation in key network industries like energy and logistics . And an unchanged monetary policy mandate for the South African Reserve Bank that prioritises inflation targeting over mass employment . <p> " Austerity has created a vicious cycle in which expenditure cuts suppress growth . Slower growth reduces revenue , and declining revenue leads to further cuts , " the statement read . This cycle , the party argues , has trapped the economy in stagnation . It has also deepened the suffering of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ grant <p> The SACP saved particular scorn for a proposal to link the Social Relief of Distress Grant to employment-seeking activities . This grant is a vital lifeline for millions of South Africans with no other income . The party called the idea " both irrational and unjust " . It argues that it " punishes the unemployed for an economic crisis they did not create " . And in an economy that simply does not generate enough jobs . <p> " The economy has not been generating employment on any meaningful scale to overcome the unemployment crisis , " the statement noted . It pointed to graduate unemployment of 12.2% as evidence of a structural , not individual , failure . <h> Break from neo-liberalism <p> The SACP 's alternative call is for a " revolutionary break " from neoliberalism . It advocates for an expansionary fiscal policy to finance public infrastructure . A central bank mandate focused on industrialisation and employment . And the strengthening of state ownership under democratic control . <p> " The economy must serve the people . And to do so it must be @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Politburo asserted . It channelled the language of the historic Freedom Charter . <p> The statement also covered international issues . It condemns United States " imperialist " aggression towards Venezuela , labelling Israeli actions in Gaza a " genocide " . And it reiterated solidarity with Cuba , Western Sahara , and pro-democracy activists in Swaziland .

@@131341431 <h> OPINION <h> The BBC in crisis <p> Andrew Donaldson <p> 12 November 2025 <p> Andrew Donaldson on Britain 's troubled public broadcaster <p> A FAMOUS GROUSE <p> ON the evening news of Sunday , 14 July , 1940 , the BBC broadcast the first ever eye-witness account of a Battle of Britain dogfight . Acting on a hunch that weekend , 28-year-old Charles Gardner , one of the Beeb 's first news reporters , travelled to Dover with the expectation of an enemy attack . STEADYPAYWALL <p> His instincts paid off : Stuka dive bombers appeared and began attacking a convoy of ships in the English Channel . RAF fighters were scrambled and , standing on the cliffs with a microphone in hand , Gardner described how they engaged with the German aircraft in aerial combat , eventually seeing off the raiders and leaving the convoy unscathed . <p> Listeners were heartened by Gardner 's blow-by-blow account . Morale in Britain was low at the time -- a German invasion was feared to be imminent -- and this was indeed welcome news . Robert Gardner , who wrote a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Broadcaster : Charles Gardner , Radio Pioneer and WWII Pilot ( Air World , 2019 ) , later noted that the public were so uplifted by the broadcast , the BBC felt compelled to repeat the item the following day . <p> Unfortunately , it also sparked a national controversy . Gardner 's account was heavily criticised by some for being " too excitable " , especially when describing a German aircraft being downed . There were complaints that his commentary was " like a football match " and entirely inappropriate when men 's lives were at stake . There were questions in the House of Commons , and it was felt that Gardner 's job was to report on events -- and not comment on them . <p> Thus an indication that criticism of the Beeb over lack of " impartiality " or bias in its news reporting is not new . That charge has however now been amped to the nth degree with the Trump administration accusing the corporation of being a corrupt " propaganda machine " and , irony of ironies , peddling " 100 per cent @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ though , is that it remains the most trusted news organisation in the world . No other international media operation can match Auntie 's global reach . Its World Service broadcasts to more than 200 countries and territories through various platforms , including the BBC World News TV channel . Domestically , BBC News reaches 74 per cent of UK adults in a week -- three times the reach of any broadcaster in the US , where distrust of news brands is growing . <p> Trust in Auntie is predicated on a high standard of impartiality in its news journalism . Its intentions here , honourable if at times matronly and stuffy , often seemed extreme . I remember , back in the days of PW Botha 's emergency , the Beeb axed journalist Sarah Crowe after she was filmed saluting with a clenched fist while mourners sang a struggle song at a township funeral . Protestations from colleagues that it would have been unwise and dangerous not to have done so and incur the wrath of volatile comrades angered at such " disrespect " came to nought ; Crowe @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ seen the farce of the Beeb moving heaven and earth to interview flat earth climate change deniers to " balance " reports on global warming , or finding crackpot businessmen to counter widespread opinion from economists on the harmful effects of Brexit . <p> Which brings us to the BBC 's current crisis . The corporation is under vociferous attack , an assault that some have perhaps rashly framed as a right wing coup attempt following the publication in the Daily Telegraph of a " devastating " internal dossier of incidents of apparent bias . <p> --&gt; <p> Conspiracy theories aside , this document , prepared by former political journalist Michael Prescott , is centred on three serious areas of transgression by the BBC , the fallout of which has resulted in the resignation of director general Tim Davie and news head Deborah Turness . This , it 's feared , is just the start of a pile-on and Auntie is now bracing itself for a right kicking from all sorts of politicos baying for blood . <p> The first of its mistakes was the BBC board 's lack of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and elsewhere . Perhaps the most egregious example here was the screening of Gaza : How to Survive a Warzone , a documentary , it later emerged , had been narrated by the 13-year-old son of a Hamas deputy minister . <p> The Beeb faced criticism and a sanction for failing to disclose this family connection to the audience at the time of the initial broadcast , a " serious breach " of editorial guidelines on accuracy . The documentary was subsequently removed from its streaming platform but later re-uploaded with an on-screen message clarifying the boy 's family link . <p> Then there are concerns with the BBC 's coverage of transgender issues , specifically that a unit of rogue reporters is censoring coverage in this regard . As Prescott writes : <p> --&gt; <p> " I was told that time and time again the LGBTQ desk staffers would decline to cover any story raising difficult questions about the trans-debate . The allegation made to me was stark : that the desk had been captured by a small group of people promoting the Stonewall view of the debate and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ lack their own reporters as a counterweight . " <p> The issue was highlighted this week by the farce now known as " the eye roll incident " . Newsreader Maxine Croxall was censured by the corporation 's Executive Complaints Unit for changing the phrase " pregnant people " on her autocue script to " women " during a bulletin in June this year . This impromptu amendment was accompanied by what appeared to be the faint rolling of eyes . <p> Outrage of a confected sort followed . Although Croxall 's actions were praised by high-profile women like Martina Navratilova and JK Rowling , along with the support of the newsreader 's bosses , the BBC received a reported 428 complaints , including a number that came from BBC staffers . This prompted an inquiry with ECU boss Fraser Steel ruling that she had breached editorial guidelines : <p> " The facial expression which accompanied the change of ' people ' to ' women ' laid it open to the interpretation that it indicated a particular viewpoint in the controversies currently surrounding trans identity . Giving the strong impression @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ even if inadvertently , falls short of the BBC 's expectations of its presenters and journalists in relation to impartiality . " <p> The most serious charge in the Prescott dossier concerns the now infamous " Panorama documentary edit " -- using two separate elements of a speech given by Donald Trump to his supporters on 6 January , 2021 , to give the impression that he had incited protestors to storm the Capitol . The BBC has admitted and apologised for what it termed an " error of judgment " . <p> Spliced together by the programme 's producers , it appeared as if Trump had said : " We 're gon na walk down to the Capitol and I 'll be with you and we fight . We fight like hell and if you do n't fight like hell , you 're not gon na have a country anymore . " <p> The truth is that the first part of that quote -- " We 're gon na walk down to the Capitol and I 'll be with you " -- came 15 minutes into Trump 's speech @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . We fight like hell ... " -- came almost an hour later . What he actually said was : " We are gon na walk down to the Capitol and I 'll be with you . I know that everyone here will soon be marching over to the Capitol building to peacefully and patriotically make your voices heard . " <p> Prescott 's dossier does n't explain the context of the " fight like hell " part of the speech . But no matter . Commentators have pointed out that the " edit " was utterly unnecessary ; there was no need to mislead viewers in this regard . As the Guardian columnist John Crace noted : <p> " Everyone already knew that Trump did n't accept the result of a democratically held election . There was no need to lay it on any thicker . People understood exactly what The Donald thought . He had been hiding in plain sight . Yet Panorama tampered with the footage and inevitably got caught out . As was bound to happen . After all , it was n't even that subtle @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ BBC until Friday afternoon to issue a retraction of the programmes , apologise and compensate him , failing which he will be obliged to sue for defamation . He wants damages of a billion dollars . <p> At the time of writing , this was still very much just a threat . If a lawsuit does come , it wo n't however be launched in the UK . British libel law , which does n't usually favour claimants , is based on the notion that a person 's reputation has suffered as a result of publication . <p> In Trump 's case , he would need to prove that the Panorama documentary had not only harmed his reputation but must also quantify this damage . As one lawyer told SkyNews : " The key factor is whether the reasonable viewer would believe the video to be true and not realise it has been falsified -- the meaning of the statement , as conveyed by the video , will be crucial in determining whether it is defamatory . " <p> As for damages , and should it come to that , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the alleged harm to Trump 's reputation caused by the editing of a video clip in a documentary screened in the UK shortly before he won a landslide presidential election victory . A reputation , we should add , already marked by , among other things , a felony conviction on 34 counts of falsifying business records to pay hush money to a porn star and a civil case in which he was ordered to pay $83 million to a woman who accused him of sexual assault . <p> There is also the inconvenient matter that the statute of limitations for libel in the UK is one year from the date of the defamatory statement 's first publication . Panorama 's Trump : A Second Chance ? was screened on October 24 last year , so work that out . <p> It does seem problematic . But then , as I 've noted before , I am not a legal expert . And this is probably why , Trump 's lawyers have suggested , his lawsuit will be launched in Florida . This raises all sorts of jurisdictional issues . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ such , could it have affected Trump 's reputation ? <p> I personally doubt whether there will be a lawsuit . But this hardly matters . The damage the BBC has already suffered as a result of the mere threat of litigation by the US president does seem immense . If it is to restore its reputation as an independent , impartial and trustworthy broadcaster , then it needs to get back to the basics of journalism . That is , do n't make stuff up . Ignore the critics and get on with the job . <p> Painful lessons <p> Eye rolling may however be in order here . That , at least , was the reaction from some of the regulars at the Slaughtered Lamb ( " Finest Ales &amp; Pies " ) to the news that more than 20 South African men were sent to Russia by Jacob Zuma 's MK Party for training to become bodyguards for party nobs -- only to wind up " trapped in the war-torn Donbas " region of Ukraine after being " lured to join mercenary forces ... under the pretext @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is in possession of a letter , dated September 19 , which bears what appears to be the Blesser 's signature , imploring Russian defence minister Andrey Belousov to pull the men from the frontline and send them to a safe location . <p> " Your excellency , " Convict Number One writes , " while our resolve to support the Russian Federation remains unshaken , I must humbly and urgently appeal to you directly . Sending these untrained , inexperienced volunteers into active combat is a certain tragedy . It would be a devastating loss to their families , our party , and to the future of Russo-African relations . " <p> He added that these men were sent to Russia to " learn from the world 's finest , so that they may one day return to Africa as capable leaders and steadfast champions of our common cause " . Instead , they were given three weeks training and tossed into the fray as cannon fodder . <p> " The gravity of this situation is compounded by the fact that Western media actors have already made contact with @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ from this misunderstanding , " the letter continues . " We are currently suppressing this information , but our ability to do so is limited , and time is critically short . " <p> What then have these unfortunate men learnt from the " world 's finest " ? Well , obviously , that Russians ca n't be trusted . And maybe how it is that , despite the human cost , Vladimir Putin can continue waging this war . The actual explanations came from Arabic-language Social media groups used by Gazans . As the plane sat on the tarmac , family members and neighbours posted clarifications and shared their experiences . <p> They described a structured exit process managed through Al-Majd Europe . Applicants register online , wait for confirmation , then pay between $1700 and $2000 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are submitted to the Israeli coordination office , and applicants wait for Israel to approve them . Once clearance comes through , Al-Majd gives final instructions and arranges the movement to Ramon Airport inside Israel . The accounts were consistent . They showed a lawful , predictable pathway that ordinary Gazans knew existed . Most importantly , they made it clear these individuals wanted to leave Gaza . <p> This was not the South African story . It was the product of a ceasefire framework and an organised , voluntary exit mechanism . The Gazans paid for this process to exercise their own choice , not because of any sudden emergency or abandonment . <p> How the exit pipeline actually works <p> Inside Gaza , the mechanism functions quietly . Applicants begin with online registration through Al-Majd Europe . Their documentation is collected through intermediaries embedded in everyday institutions such as civil offices and Al-Aqsa Hospital . At the hospital , it is often cleaners or administrative staff who guide applicants toward the correct internal contacts . <p> Once registration is complete , Al-Majd places the applicant into the coordination @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , the details go to the Israeli coordination office for vetting . Israel confirms identities , conducts security assessments , and verifies the stated purpose of travel . Nothing moves forward without Israeli approval . <p> When approval is granted , applicants receive instructions and are transported out of Gaza , through Israeli-controlled checkpoints , and to Ramon Airport . The departure is processed in Israel , and only then does the onward journey begin . The entire chain depends on Israeli authorisation and coordination at every stage . <p> The ceasefire framework that made this possible <p> The Sharam El-Sheikh agreement , negotiated earlier this year , forms the foundation of this entire mechanism . It was concluded between the United States , Egypt , Qatar , and Turkey , with over twenty states participating in the broader framework . It established a structured process allowing Gazans to leave voluntarily and safely , with Israel responsible for operational coordination in partnership with these international actors . <p> South Africa had no role in this framework . The Palestinian Authority did not implement or administer it either , even though @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ were aware of the voluntary exit provisions . Their absence in South Africa 's information chain stems not from exclusion , but from the fact that they were not operational participants . They simply never conveyed this information to Pretoria . <p> Why the South African narrative collapsed <p> South African commentators claimed Israel had blocked or abandoned the travellers . This is impossible when Israel is the state actor that approved , vetted , and physically enabled their departure . Hamas refuses to let its population leave . Israel permitted it . <p> COGAT publicly confirms the exit mechanisms <p> Israel 's Coordination of Government Activities in the Territories ( COGAT ) publicly affirmed the broader exit mechanisms operating out of Gaza , reinforcing exactly what Gazans themselves described . On 13 November , COGAT released an official statement confirming that close to 250 Gazans in need of medical treatment , along with caregivers and dual citizenship holders , exited Gaza through the coordinated crossings at Kerem Shalom , the <p> Allenby Bridge , and the Ramon Airport . They highlighted that these movements form part of an ongoing @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ continues to work with global partners to facilitate the safe medical evacuation of civilians . This aligns directly with the structured , vetted , and internationally coordinated process that enabled the group to reach South Africa . It also demonstrates that the movement of Gazans out of Gaza is neither sudden nor mysterious . It is part of an established humanitarian and administrative system that Israel manages transparently and routinely in partnership with other states . <p> This public confirmation stands in stark contrast to the claims circulated in South Africa . Far from blocking travellers or abandoning them , Israel was actively enabling the secure passage of civilians through the very same mechanism . The social-media narrative in South Africa ignored Israel 's documented role in approving , coordinating , and securing these movements . COGAT 's statement reinforces the factual reality : these were organised , lawful exits conducted through official channels with clear humanitarian purpose and international oversight . <h> The Ramon to Nairobi route <p> From Ramon Airport , the group was routed through Nairobi as a routine logistical connection . Global Aviation Flight 901 left @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and returned early on the 13th carrying the Gazan travellers . Kenya did not stamp their passports because they were in transit . Israel did not stamp passports because Israel discontinued passport stamping years ago to protect travellers from discrimination in countries that penalise entry from Israel . Instead , Israel issues electronic entry cards . <p> Inside South Africa , these ordinary travel procedures were twisted into accusations . Gift of the Givers publicly claimed Israel had refused to stamp passports . Activists echoed the claim . Officials wandered into the same messaging . The details of transit procedures were discarded and replaced with a narrative that matched domestic political agendas . <p> What really happened on the ground <p> The scene inside OR Tambo was far less dramatic than the online version , but the handling was deeply flawed . Immigration officers attempted to verify identities and travel histories while passengers sat on the grounded aircraft for hours without proper food , water , or ventilation . Children and a heavily pregnant woman became distressed . This was not a crisis caused by Israel . It was the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> Throughout the day , officials issued contradictory statements . By the time the travellers were allowed to disembark , the country had already been swept up in a completely false narrative . <p> Gift of the Givers ' Opportunistic Intervention and the Distortion of State Response <p> Gift of the Givers had no role in organising any part of this movement . They did not arrange the exit , the coordination , the transit , or the aircraft . They only appeared once the plane had already landed . <p> Yet a single call from Imtiaz Sooliman triggered a rapid shift inside government . DIRCO , the Presidency , and various spokespeople aligned their messaging with his public statements , despite those statements being factually wrong and based on a misunderstanding or deliberate reframing of the process . <p> This exposed a significant weakness in South Africa 's governance . Officials relied on the narrative of one unelected NGO figure rather than verified intelligence . It raises serious questions about how one individual can influence policy posture across multiple departments in a single phone call , what precedent @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ appears more responsive to a public personality than to its own information networks . <p> This was not humanitarian leadership . It was institutional fragility . At least one previous Ramon movement passed through the same route quietly , unnoticed by both the government and Gift of the Givers . The issue was South Africa 's lack of information , not Israel 's actions . <p> South Africans deserve a state grounded in facts , not a state that adopts unverified narrative as official doctrine . <p> The human reality <p> Strip away the noise . These travellers were ordinary people who made a voluntary decision to leave Gaza through a lawful mechanism . Their movement was structured , coordinated , and authorised under an international framework that South Africans had never been told about . That silence allowed others to turn human lives into political theatre . <p> The tragedy behind the noise <p> The tragedy was never their arrival . The tragedy was how quickly South Africa turned a routine movement into a spectacle engineered for outrage . A lawful exit pipeline was repackaged as crisis . Facts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ truth emerges , the damage will have taken hold . South Africans were fed a story that bore no resemblance to the actual process . A vacuum of transparency allowed misinformation to flourish . <p> Beyond the misinformation <p> This episode reveals a deeper structural problem . South Africa 's political environment is so vulnerable to reactive storytelling that a single influential voice can override due process and distort public understanding . Officials responded to headlines , not facts . Institutions deferred to NGO messaging instead of international documentation . <p> The result was predictable confusion and administrative paralysis . <p> This was not a humanitarian emergency . It was a failure of state competence . Final Reflection <p> The Ramon Corridor exposed how easily national understanding can be shaped by those who speak the loudest rather than those who know the facts . It revealed a government that can be swayed by public personalities more quickly than by verified intelligence . And it showed how misinformation becomes policy when truth is treated as optional . <p> This investigation returns the facts to the centre . It cuts through the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ individuals left Gaza , how they reached South Africa , and how their story was rewritten by those who never understood it . <p> They deserved better . So did the public . This report ensures they finally receive the truth .
